# TOPAK — Feature List

**2026.11.11** · derived · drawn from Genesis Checkpoint G11 (2026.10.09), Theory of Operation revision 15 (with its
2026.10.12 descriptor edit) and the record files at commit `bbda621`, which carries Batches 38 and 39 and thirteen
records drafted after G11 (ADR-0056 to ADR-0068, all Proposed) · regenerates `topak-feature-list-2026.11.05.md`,
which it supersedes as basis · Playbook marks are `topak-external-alignment-2026.10.09.md`'s · one of five documents
of the self review of 2026.11.11 (`topak-critical-review-2026.11.11.md` is its head) · prepared by the assistant at
the custodian's request

A feature list derived from the state of record and from what is proposed beyond it, in three parts. **Part I** is
the principles, each now with what the thirteen Proposed records would do to it. **Part II** is the features: the
607 rows of the 11.05 list, every one re-checked against this tree, and 356 new rows for the records and artifacts
that arrived after it. **Part III** is what distinguishes the pattern, where the field is ahead, the Playbook
correspondence, what moved since the 11.05 list, and the stale sentences found on the way.

**Basis and limits.**

- **Nothing here is ratified, and a derived document ratifies nothing.** G11 is still the state of record. No
  checkpoint has been cut since 2026.10.09 and no batch ratified since Batch 39 (2026.11.03).
- **Every carried row was re-checked, not copied.** Five graders, each a fresh context with read-only access to the
  tree, took the 607 rows by section and checked each against the files: the record's Status line and History rows,
  the artifact behind every *Proved*, *Certified*, *Checked* and *Built*, the open-list item, and the numbers in
  the row's first sentence. 544 rows stand as the 11.05 list has them, 33 moved after that list's tree, and 30 were
  already untrue at it and are corrected here (§17). The graders' work was not itself verified by a second reader.
  A cell the 11.05 list cut with "…" was checked only as far as its visible text.
- **The new rows are drawn from Proposed records.** A pending amendment (fifteen at this tree) is listed as
  **Pending** and counts for nothing. Not one new row is in force.
- **The overlay's rows are an overlay's.** ADR-0059 to ADR-0068 say of themselves that they are "an overlay, not
  the archetype" ⟨Q11⟩. Their 212 rows are in §12, apart from the archetype's, and each status begins
  `Proposed (overlay)`. A one-plane deployment takes nothing from them.
- **Record against module.** Where a new row's record states something its module states differently, or not at
  all, the row is drawn from the record and the Note says so. The review's register carries those differences as
  findings; this list does not grade them.
- **The principles are still a synthesis**, the drafter's, as the 11.05 list said of its own.
- **Every text since G10 was drafted by the assistant** at the custodian's direction and reviewed by no reader other
  than the drafter. So was this list. No bootstrap reviewer is engaged and no review pass has been run (G11 §4).
- **The words.** *Proved* is said of a module's invariant under the prover's hypotheses and the module's
  assumptions; *proved in Lean* of a re-encoding, a transcription, a model or a statement under Cedar's model, and
  is never shortened; *checked* of TLC at small constants; none of them of the running reference.
- **Cells are abbreviated**, as in the 11.05 list. The new rows' full text and the graders' evidence for every
  carried row are in the annex: `topak-self-review-annex-2026.11.11/feature-rows-new.tsv` and
  `feature-rows-rechecked.tsv`.

**Status key**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Accepted** | Ratified record, genesis phase; a `C1` to `C4` marks a confirmation-required criterion (ADR-0029 §4); **provisional** marks a record in force and subject to redesign (ADR-0029 A4) |
| **Proposed** | A record or section drafted and not ratified (ADR-0035, 0036, 0051, 0054, 0055; ADR-0022 §6) |
| **Pending** | An amendment to an accepted record, marked inline and not applied |
| **Built** | Exists at the reference under a Proposed or pending text; cited by no record in force |
| **Proved** | **(TLAPS)**: a TLA+ invariant proved by the prover and checked by TLC (ADR-0046), of the module and not the code. **(Lean)**: proved by the Lean kernel, of a re-encoding, a transcription, a model or a statement under Cedar's model, counted with its replay (ADR-0053). **(both)**: by each. *Proved in Lean* is never shortened to *proved* in prose |
| **Certified** | Every SMT proof accepted by the pinned checker and every Zenon proof by Isabelle (ADR-0046 A1); *not certified* and *certified in part* say which modules fall short. A certified proof is not a verified one (G11 §8) |
| **Checked** | Checked by TLC at small constants and proved by nothing |
| **Stated** | The floor's text stated under Cedar's verified model; a claim under the model, not a finding of fitness (ADR-0053 §4) |
| **Declared** | A position a record states and nothing enforces: a label, a lineage tag, a rung, a value |
| **Practice** | A convention the corpus follows, with the tooling that holds it where there is one |
| **Derived** | A consequence stated in the narrative with no record of its own |
| **Open** | Named on G11's open list, by item number; no decision |
| **SIMULATED** | Run at the reference under test keys standing in for trust roots; claims nothing (ADR-0033 A7) |
| **Reported upstream** | A defect in a trust root reported to its maintainer, and recorded by amendment (ADR-0046 A3) |
| **Withdrawn** · **Reserved** · **Superseded** | A drafted amendment withdrawn and never in force (ADR-0026 W1); a record number held with no file (ADR-0014); a record no longer in force, naming its successor (ADR-0008) |
| **Proposed (overlay)** | The overlay records' own position (ADR-0059 to ADR-0068, ⟨Q11⟩): drafted as an overlay's and not the archetype's. ADR-0029 §3 has no such status, and ADR-0058, itself Proposed, defines an overlay as a deployment's graph; the words are the records' |
| **not built** | Nothing at the reference follows the text |
| **Corrected** (§17 only) | The 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree, and is restated here |

**Playbook mark key:** M matched · P partial · C convertible · G gap · X out of scope, as the 10.09 alignment defines
them. Its tally at G11 is M 11 · P 27 · C 3 · G 3 · X 28 over 72 rows; §15 says which rows Batches 38 and 39 would
move.

# Part I — Principles

The 11.05 list split the principles into ten **endemic** to the design and nine **exogenous** to it, and gave for
each what had changed since G9. That column is replaced here by what the thirteen Proposed records would do to the
principle if they were ratified. The statements are the 11.05 list's, at this tree: none of them has changed,
because nothing was ratified. The numbers still collide with the confinement profile's clauses and
`RulesFloorNV`'s properties, so a principle is always written with the word: principle P9.

## The endemic principles: as designed

| # | Principle | Statement at this tree | What ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 would do to it (all Proposed) | Basis | Carried by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P1** | **Verify the cage, not the occupant** | An agent is Byzantine at every position, so every guarantee is stated against what its alphabet allows and proved of the mechanism's modules, never of the model; semantic judgment is never a lift. What runs is evidence, not the verified object, and is a trusted-base row until a refinement exists | Extended, not reversed. `spec/` holds thirty-two proof modules, eight of them the overlay's and cited only by Proposed records (F1.6). ADR-0057 would make T-0045-28 a derived premise with routes, inherited at a grade. ADR-0062's governor is a trusted component that permits away from the replicas, which strains "proved of the mechanism's modules" | ADR-0007, ADR-0010, ADR-0033 §1, ADR-0045 T-0045-28, ADR-0046, ADR-0053; ToO §1, §2.1 to §2.3 | §1 |
| **P2** | **Monotone authority, fail closed** | Authority changes only by promotion or anchor issuance; grants need consensus and subtractions do not; every layer only subtracts, and the corpus states that at eighteen layers; every failure lands on deny or stall | Strained in four places. ADR-0056: a subject bound at grant carries authority that is its provider's. ADR-0060: an intent is one operator's grant. ADR-0062: a `local` permit has no ordered admit, and "deny or stall" gains hold and a voted terminal behaviour. ADR-0061: a membership step by schedule sits beside "a timeout is never a grant" | ADR-0015, ADR-0023, ADR-0030, ADR-0031, ADR-0044, ADR-0050; ToO §7.8 | §2 |
| **P3** | **One containment component, two domains** | `Build(Contain) → Run(Contain)`: one component instantiated twice, each domain with its own typed alphabet sharing only the carrier; the Byzantine threat lives in Build, which authors what Run is judged against | Keeps its statement. ADR-0062, ADR-0065 and ADR-0067 would owe Run's alphabet, cell and policy records their entries (§17) | ADR-0001, ADR-0002, ADR-0040 to ADR-0043; ToO §1 | §3 |
| **P4** | **Universal, evictable principals** | One principal type for agents and humans, one seat each; a principal is anything evictable by an authority other than itself; the lease is its only credential | "The lease is its only credential" would need the subject beside it (ADR-0056). A `release` seat kind, held by a person, is added (ADR-0066). A plane is seated by its parent's promotion under a charter, so a mechanism grants below itself (ADR-0059) | ADR-0002, ADR-0031, ADR-0037, ADR-0050, ADR-0052; ToO §2.8, §7.10 | §4 |
| **P5** | **Oversight placed by consistency budget** | HITL, HOTL and HOOTL follow from each domain's budget `W + L + D < T_irrev` and are `ENABLED` predicates; clocks buy liveness and never safety; an effect too fast to stop must be inexpressible | The hardest hit. "An effect too fast to stop must be inexpressible" becomes reachable by a person at a release seat (ADR-0066). The budget cannot be met while a plane is unattended, and ADR-0060 §5 leaves three options undecided (R2). ADR-0063's bound is in effects (`K_up`) where the budget is in time, and ADR-0064 puts a premise about time under a declared bound | ADR-0032; glossary §7; ToO §11.3; ADR-0050, ADR-0052 | §5 |
| **P6** | **Privileged paths are modeled events** | Erasure, the emergency seating, revocation and the fence are transitions in the specification with properties, mutations and proofs, never side doors; the floor carries one exception, erasure | Extended: a release act and a plane fence are modelled events with properties and mutations (`Release`, `Federation`) | ADR-0006, ADR-0009, ADR-0013, ADR-0025, ADR-0026, ADR-0028, ADR-0052 | §6 |
| **P7** | **Independent observation, bounded evidence** | Detection is per cell, off the Thinker's fault domain, deny-only and any-of, and coupled to admission so that absence stalls; collusion is the declared gap | Coupling moves from the admit to the grant for a `local` entry, and whether a cell's Observer sits on another vehicle is declared per mission (ADR-0062 §8, ⟨Q9⟩). Revocation below the intent boundary is bounded in effects, with the boundary as the plane's Observer (ADR-0063) | ADR-0038, ADR-0039, ADR-0051; ToO §5 | §7 |
| **P9** | **Ingress is labelled by source, never judged by content** | What a Run Thinker reads carries a label lifted from its declared source; what is suppressed arrives as a handle with no bytes; three floor forbids bound what may go outward after what was read; nothing in the mechanism reads content to make a label | Extended: a peer's report is a labelled item, `untrusted`, and nothing a peer originates enables anything (ADR-0065). ADR-0056 touches it only if its open obligation on labelling a subject's data is taken | ADR-0047, ADR-0048, ADR-0049; ToO §2.10 | §9 |
| **P10** | **Composition by promotion, not delegation** (Proposed) | No principal seats, delegates to or addresses another: a workflow is a promoted graph whose promotion seats every cell, the mechanism grants each node's lease, and the one cell that chooses what runs next may only select among edges already enabled | Its words stand, by ADR-0068 §4. Its name and ToO §2.11's sentence are reversed in letter: ADR-0059 says of itself "it is still delegation". One sentence of ADR-0055 §7 would go (ADR-0065) | ADR-0054, ADR-0055; ToO §2.11 and its edit after the `ToO-rev15` tag | §10 |
| **P11** | **Trust admitted by failure direction** | A component, a clock or a check whose failure can only subtract may be trusted for nothing; one whose failure can add authority enters only if its identity is issued, and is otherwise refused or brought inside the verified object | ADR-0068's Findings 1 names four rows that meet neither of ADR-0045's tests as they stand: a vehicle's measure of an extent, its position source, its interlock and its recovery function. Under ADR-0058 a deployment's own prover would enter only as a degeneracy, "which reads as a weakening even where it strengthens" (its Findings 2) | ADR-0045 §2, §6; ADR-0032 §2; ADR-0030 A1; ADR-0034 (rung 3 as the archetype); ToO §7.9 | §1, §2, §5 |

## The exogenous principles: by process

| # | Principle | Statement at this tree | What ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 would do to it (all Proposed) | Basis | Carried by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P8.1** | **The corpus governs itself, and its debt is on the face** | Custody is declared, (1, 0); the custodian ratifies unilaterally; a record carrying a privileged-path, exhaustiveness, reversal or debt-resolving claim is confirmation-required and cited as author-ratified; a record that missed its own ratification condition may be in force as provisional, with its exit stated; bootstrap needs an independent review of the whole corpus, and is permission, not obligation | Keeps its statement. The overlay records claim a position, "an overlay, not the archetype", and a suspension of C3 while it lasts; ADR-0029 §3 and §4 define neither (the review's findings H-2, B-21 and I2-1) | ADR-0029 §2, §4, A2, A4; ToO §9; `.claude/settings.json`; `CODEOWNERS` | §8.1 |
| **P8.2** | **Records change only by typed history, and cuts are never revised** | A record is never edited after ratification except by a typed History row; a checkpoint is one cut, never revised, with its errors recorded forward; a frozen module gets a successor or an extension, never an edit; a clause that can be superseded alone is a separate record | Would gain the clause's four parts and numbered guarantees (ADR-0057 §2). Batches 38 and 39 still stand only as History rows, in no checkpoint | `decisions/README.md`; `checkpoints/README.md`; G11 §5; the decomposition test, `narrative/revisions/topak-theory-of-operation-2026.09.12.md` | §8.2 |
| **P8.3** | **Self-measuring: every cut measured, every tool pinned, drift caught by tooling** | Each checkpoint and reference tree is measured by a manifest that verifies at its own commit; every tool a result rests on is pinned by digest and printed in its results file; pins and sync scripts fail when something moves under what cites it | Whole-file pins would become clause pins, and four lists would be read against one graph (ADR-0057). At this tree the graph has no node for ADR-0059 to ADR-0068, and 190 tracked files are measured by no manifest | `manifests/README.md`; `tools/verify-manifest.sh`; `mechanism/corpus-pins.sha256`; `spec/proof-sync.sh`; `tools/lean-sync.sh`; `tools/check-refs.py` | §8.3 |
| **P8.4** | **Execute the record** | A ratification condition names what must be run and seen: gates regenerated, mutations that must each break their property, witnesses that must be reached, behaviours seen at the reference; a check that cannot fail is refused; what running code finds goes back to the record that owns it | Keeps its statement. The overlay's eight modules follow it for TLC and TLAPS; for the floor and the reference its conditions are unmet, and nothing is built | ADR-0046 §1; ADR-0030 §5; ADR-0033; ToO §9, §15; G11 §3 | §8.4 |
| **P8.5** | **Exact words for evidence** | *Checked*, *proved*, *proved in Lean*, *certified*, *stated*, *built*, *simulated*, *provisional* and *author-ratified* each mean one thing and are never shortened; none of them means reviewed, and none is said of the running reference | Would gain eight edge-type words and a grade on every claim (ADR-0057 §3, §5), one of which, the type *proved*, also covers Lean and gate results | ADR-0046 §1; ADR-0053 §1; glossary §13; ToO §0, §13 A8 and A17 | §8.5 |
| **P8.6** | **Check the checker** | A verifier is a trusted-base row that can fail: its answer counts only with a checked proof or a fresh replay, its configuration is pinned, and a second method proves again what the first proved and certifies nothing new | Keeps its statement | ADR-0030 A1; ADR-0045 T-0045-14, 29, 31, 37; ADR-0046 A1; ADR-0053 §1, §5, R7 | §8.6 |
| **P8.7** | **Defects in a trust root are disclosed** | A trust root found to fail keeps its class; the defect is recorded against its row with the rule that avoids it, every cited result is re-read against it, the reproducers are kept as files, and the defect is reported to its maintainer | Keeps its statement. The upstream report (Batch 39) is still unsaid in the narrative and in G11 item 104 | ADR-0046 A2, A3; ADR-0045 A3, A4; `spec/proof-certification.md` §8, §10, §12 | §8.7 |
| **P8.8** | **One hand is not independence, and it is said on every face** | Every record, proof, module and line of code is one person's working with an assistant; every assisted text says so, every ratification row says it was not reviewed by another reader, and every checkpoint says no bootstrap reviewer is engaged | Keeps its statement, on more: thirteen records, eight modules and their proofs since G11 are one hand's, ten of the records in one day | ADR-0029 A2.5; `checkpoints/README.md`; G11 §1.2, §4, §8; ToO §0, §12 item 3 | §8.8 |
| **P8.9** | **Residual on the face** | What a record owes, closes and does not claim is stated where the claim is made; every number without a measurement says it is a placeholder; every trusted-base row carries a residual; every checkpoint ends with what it does not claim; outside systems are compared by dated readings of primary text | Keeps its statement. ADR-0068 states the overlay's residuals on its face: fourteen adversaries, two of them bounded by nothing | ADR-0045 §1; G11 §8; ToO §12, §13; `floor/genesis-values.json`; `derived/topak-residual-risk-statement-2026.10.09.md` | §8.9 |

**What the Proposed set would do, in one place.**

- **Reversed in letter:** P10's name and ToO §2.11's sentence (ADR-0059).
- **Strained:** P2 (four places), P5 (four), P7 (where coupling sits), P1 and P11 (the governor; trust roots nothing
  issues).
- **Extended:** P4, P6, P9.
- **Kept:** P3 and the nine exogenous principles, of which P8.1, P8.2 and P8.3 are the ones the corpus is not
  meeting at this tree (the review, §4).
- **No principle for it:** adversaries that are not the agent (a captured plane, a jammer, a vehicle in hand), a
  bound in effects where P5's is in time, and the unattended posture, which is none of P5's three.

# Part II — Features

The rows of §1 to §10 are the 11.05 list's, with their ids and order, re-checked. A row that moved or was
corrected carries its new cell here and its reason in §17. §11 and §12 are new. A cell is abbreviated as before: the
feature is its first sentence, the basis names records only, and the status leads with the record's standing and
then lists what is proved, built or open. Playbook marks are the 10.09 alignment's and are shown only where a
carried row has one; the new rows have none, because no alignment has been made against them.

## 1. P1 — Verify the cage, not the occupant

The largest section, because the formal layer, confinement and the trusted base all answer to P1. At G9 it rested on fifteen proof modules; at this tree it rests on twenty-four, twenty-one of them certified, fifteen proved again in Lean, and a prover known to prove false statements in two ways, whose defects are §8.7's. What runs is still not what is proved (F1.8), and no real principal is `Contained` (F1.10, F1.12).

**At `bbda621`.** 124 rows re-checked: 111 as the 11.05 list has them, 11 moved since, 2 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 39 of them (§17). The lead above is the 11.05 list's: `spec/` now holds thirty-two proof modules (11,016 obligations, twenty-nine certified), and the eight added on 2026.11.10 are the overlay's, cited only by Proposed records.

**Carried from the 10.03 list.**

## 2. P2 — Monotone authority, fail closed

Every row here either says what adds authority or shows that something else only subtracts. Since G9 operators keep a quorum's grant going and may only narrow it, the floor in force became a stage of the proposed floor, and every declared degeneracy is gathered in one catalogue (F2.54).

**At `bbda621`.** 69 rows re-checked: 61 as the 11.05 list has them, 5 moved since, 3 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 18 of them (§17).

**Carried from the 10.03 list.**

A promotion is an admit under ADR-0024 whose votes are emissions … | ADR-0015; ADR-0045; ADR-0024; ADR-0025 | Accepted (genesis) · C2 (ADR-0015, Batch 3: both sets claimed exhaustive); Pending; Proposed | MANAGE 2.4 M |

## 3. P3 — One containment component, two domains

Run's four records, Proposed or pending at G9, are all in force: the alphabet, the policy and mechanism obligations, the cell obligations and, since Batch 30, Run's confinement. A fourth Run variant, `Route`, is pending on ADR-0054 and is §10's.

**At `bbda621`.** 25 rows re-checked: 21 as the 11.05 list has them, 3 moved since, 1 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 9 of them (§17).

| ID | Feature | Basis | Status | Playbook |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F3.1 | **`Build(Contain) → Run(Contain)`: one containment component instantiated in two domains; Build is the hostile domain.** `Build` produces artifacts and `Run` operates them. The arrow is authority flow; the parentheses are a refinement boundary (specification conjunction plus …) | ADR-0001; ADR-0002; ADR-0010; ToO rev 15 §1 | Accepted (genesis) (ADR-0001 and ADR-0002, both 2026.09.12, plain: no confirmation marker) | MAP 4.2 M |
| F3.2 | **Run's alphabet: `Consult`, `Actuate`, `Report`.** Run principals act through exactly three variants: `Consult(ref)`, ingress and not an effect, `ref` resolving inside the pinned Run root; `Actuate(tool …)` | ADR-0040 §1; ADR-0015; ADR-0042 §1; ToO rev 15 §2.9 | Accepted (genesis) · C3, C4 (ADR-0040, Batch 20, 2026.09.30); Built; Pending | MAP 3.3 M |
| F3.3 | **Run's policy obligations: one envelope per variant; coupling on effects only; `Consult` never coupled.** The Run half of the floor is one envelope per variant: `R1-actuate-allowlisted`, `R2-report-own-topic` and `R4-consult-pinned` (new) … | ADR-0041 §1; ADR-0040 §6; ToO rev 15 §2.9; ADR-0056 §4 | Accepted (genesis) · C1, C4 (ADR-0041, Batch 20); with a provisional amendment; Certified; Pending; Proposed (ADR-0056 §4: `R10`, `R11` and the subject attributes of `RunCtx`) | MANAGE 2.3 M |
| F3.4 | **Run's mechanism obligations: the sequence counts effects, every `Actuate` leaves a record, a tool's result is ingress.** ADR-0042 §1 to §4. Every Run action carries epoch and lease and the validator is total over the three variants … | ADR-0042 §1; ADR-0039 §1; ToO rev 15 §2.9; mechanism/ I28 | Accepted (genesis) · C4 (ADR-0042 §1-§5, Batch 20); with a provisional amendment; Certified; Built | MANAGE 4.3 P |
| F3.5 | **Run's confinement obligation: channel fidelity in place of P4, in force.** For Run principals the mechanism must admit no residual channel: no shell, no network egress from the guest, no filesystem outside the Run root … | ADR-0042 §6; ADR-0040 §3; ADR-0022 §6; ADR-0034 A2; ADR-0056 §5 | Accepted (genesis) · C2 for §6 (Batch 30, 2026.10.04); Certified; Built; SIMULATED; Proposed (ADR-0056 §5, §8: the tool host would also inject the round's one credential, widening T-0045-25; C2) | MAP 4.2 M |
| F3.6 | **Build emits to the effect stream: a Build round is one pipeline invocation, sealed at submission.** Build's `Contain` emits under ADR-0003's round-topic scheme, unchanged in granularity, access control, replay authorization and sealing discipline. | ADR-0021; ADR-0003; ADR-0024 §3; ToO rev 15 §5.2 | Accepted (genesis) · C4 (ADR-0021, Batch 18, 2026.09.30: ADR-0039 §3 rests on its seal); Built | MEASURE 2.4 P |
| F3.7 | **Build's regress terminates on a verified object plus a declared axiom: three stages of one argument.** The corpus has stated the end of Build's trust regress three ways, staged as one argument: a declared axiom set (ADR-0002, 09.12); a verified artifact … | ADR-0002; ADR-0010; ADR-0012; ADR-0025 | Accepted (genesis) (ADR-0002, 0010, 0012, 0025, 0026, all plain) | GOVERN 4.2 P |
| F3.8 | **The carrier is shared; no variant name crosses domains.** Every action in either domain carries epoch and lease and is refused under a fenced epoch by the mechanism, ahead of policy … | ADR-0040 §2; ADR-0022 §2; ToO rev 15 §2.9 | Accepted (genesis) · C3, C4 (ADR-0040); ADR-0022 A2 ratified Batch 20 under C3 (its History row); Proved (both); Certified; Built | MAP 3.3 M |

## 4. P4 — Universal, evictable principals

Five seat kinds are in force: thinker, observer, correlator, and since Batch 34 two that only a person holds, `operator` and `emergency`, both from provisional records and `Mediated` by design. Delegation, open at G9, is stated as a position in Proposed text (F4.10). Lineage and independence are still Proposed (ADR-0035).

**At `bbda621`.** 38 rows re-checked: 37 as the 11.05 list has them, 1 moved since, 0 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 11 of them (§17).

## 5. P5 — Oversight placed by consistency budget

The budget is still a form with its numbers undeclared. Admins became principals: operators, and an emergency seat for one person and one round. The 10.03 list cited ToO §2.6 for the oversight postures; §2.6 is the confinement section, and the definitions are the glossary's and ToO §11.3's (F5.1).

**At `bbda621`.** 26 rows re-checked: 22 as the 11.05 list has them, 0 moved since, 4 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 11 of them (§17).

## 6. P6 — Privileged paths are modeled events

Break-glass is no longer a floor exception: it is an emergency seating, a roster's quorum seating one person for one round (ADR-0052, provisional), and the floor keeps one exception, erasure, which genesis disables. R8, the TTL, was withdrawn and never in force. Erasure itself is still unbuilt.

**At `bbda621`.** 40 rows re-checked: 30 as the 11.05 list has them, 0 moved since, 10 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 4 of them (§17). The lead's last sentence stands, and F6.1 no longer says otherwise.

## 7. P7 — Independent observation, bounded evidence

Coupling is proved, and since G11 the coupling stall is shown by TLC to end, which is checked and not proved (F7.31). The correlator's function is drafted as five folds (ADR-0051, Proposed), and the Observer and the correlator are still fixtures at the reference. Collusion remains the declared gap.

**At `bbda621`.** 70 rows re-checked: 68 as the 11.05 list has them, 0 moved since, 2 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 11 of them (§17). For "since G11" in the lead read "since G10": `Coupling_live` ran on 2026.10.07.

## 8. P8.1 to P8.9 — The exogenous principles

The process features, grouped under the nine exogenous principles of Part I. Ids are F8.<principle>.<n>, and the last column names the 10.03 row a feature carries. P8.6 and P8.7 hold the process half of the formal method; the method itself is §1's.

**At `bbda621`.** 131 rows re-checked: 117 as the 11.05 list has them, 12 moved since, 2 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 8 of them (§17).

### 8.1 P8.1 The corpus governs itself, and its debt is on the face

| ID | Feature | Basis | Status | Playbook | 10.03 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 9. P9 — Ingress is labelled by source, never judged by content

New since G9: ADR-0047 to ADR-0049, ratified in Batch 34. A label is a declaration about a source, never a finding about content, and the corpus says so as an assumption of use (ToO §13 A15). The floor's three forbids are in force through the `emergency` stage.

**At `bbda621`.** 42 rows re-checked: 40 as the 11.05 list has them, 0 moved since, 2 corrected (the 11.05 row was already untrue at its own tree). ADR-0056 to ADR-0068 touch 15 of them (§17).

## 10. P10 — Composition by promotion, not delegation (Proposed)

Nothing in this section is in force. ADR-0054 and ADR-0055 are Proposed, with twelve amendments pending on them; the reference was built to both and links their floor stage at the custodian's direction; `Workflow.tla` and `Mesh.tla` are checked by TLC and unproved. The section is here because the descriptor's 2026.10.12 edit already rests on it.

## 11. ADR-0056 to ADR-0058, and what else arrived since the 11.05 list (Proposed; new rows)

Three Proposed records drafted on 2026.11.06, the tooling ADR-0057's first revision added, and what else
arrived after the 11.05 list and belongs under the exogenous principles. ADR-0056 has no module, no floor entry
and no reference behind it. ADR-0057's graph exists as a file and a checker and, at this tree, is behind the tree
it describes. ADR-0058 states its own position as Archetype, so no row here is an overlay's. The Principle column
says which principle a row answers to and, where the record's own words say so, which it strains.

## 12. The command-chain overlay, ADR-0059 to ADR-0068 (Proposed; an overlay's, and not the archetype's; new rows)

Ten Proposed records drafted on 2026.11.10 from `topak-federated-c2-design-proposal-2026.11.10b.md`, with
eight TLA+ modules checked by TLC (321 rows), proved by TLAPS (5,600 obligations) and run through certification
the same day. Nothing is built, no floor entry is written, and no Lean package covers them. Four of the records
were revised again (R2) after their modules were frozen; the rows say where a module does not state what its
record now says. **Every row here is an overlay's and not the archetype's.** ADR-0068's adversaries, trusted-base
rows and assumptions of use each have a row, as does each entry of ADR-0067's `federation` floor stage.

Four rows here meet neither as … | ADR-0068 §6, Consequences, Findings 1, Open obligations | Proposed (overlay) (C2 named) · Declared · Open ("The reading of each row against ADR-0045", and "the disposition of finding 1") | P11 (strains: "Trust roots nothing issues." They "are trusted for what their builder says … |  |

# Part III — What distinguishes the pattern

## 13. Distinguishing characteristics

Carried from the 11.05 list, whose forty-one characteristics were each re-checked at this tree: 40 stand
as written and 1 moved (11.2.28: the eight proof modules of 2026.11.10 were scanned for the prover's two
cases, and the reading Batch 37 gave the first twenty-four is not recorded for them). The Proposed records touch
32 of the forty-one; none is withdrawn by them, because none is in force. The comparables were read on
2026.10.05 and 2026.10.09 and are not re-read here.

Drawn from the 10.05 external and competitive assessment (§4 "Where TOPAK's design is alone", §11's summary table)
and the 10.09 alignment (§1.11's reverse deltas, Part IV against IBM's MCP guide), each checked against the records
at this tree. The comparables were read on those dates and are not re-read here; a claim that "no comparable" does
something is the assessment's, as of its date. Ranked by strength of basis: a record in force first, then a
provisional or Proposed record, then a derived document alone. **Design** characteristics are endemic, **process**
characteristics exogenous.

### 13.1 Design (11.1 in the 11.05 list; the row ids are carried)

| # | Characteristic | What TOPAK does | What the field does | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The policy author is Byzantine** | The threat is placed in Build, which authors the specifications, policy and floors Run is judged against, and the authority path is built around that | Every surveyed comparable trusts the policy author: an operator, a console user, an approver. The field now checks the author's proposal (Progent v3, OpenShell's prover); it does not doubt whoever accepts it | 10.05 §4 item 1, §11; ToO §1; ADR-0001, ADR-0002 |
| 2 | **Who may change what an agent may do is itself governed** | A floor change is classified by computation (equivalent, tightening, relaxation, incomparable) and a relaxation is routed to a quorum of seats with author recusal, whose roster the anchor issues and whose anchor the rules floor bounds, ordered on the log | Progent v3, OpenShell and EBL-Core compute what a change adds; in each the approver is a configuration value, in two of them settable to auto-approve | 10.05 §4 item 3, §11; ADR-0024; ADR-0030 §5, A1 |
| 3 | **A rules floor over a havoc anchor** | The authority that defines who may approve is a havoc issuer, bounded by a verified rules floor; intent is the one axiom and custody is a declaration | No comparable states what bounds the approver's authority; AgentCity's constitutional tier is the one structural analogue, and is a blockchain economy | 10.05 §4 item 2; ADR-0025, ADR-0026 |
| 4 | **One log orders every authority change, and a decision is re-checked where it is applied** | Grants, revocations, expiries, promotions, issuances and erasures are ordered in one Paxos log, the fence is the log index, and an admit decided under one epoch is void if applied under another | EBL-Core asks for both halves and mandates neither; Zhu & Wang fence per lineage; Temporal fences shards | 10.05 §4 item 4; ADR-0033 §4; ADR-0005 |
| 5 | **Legibility, not immutability** | Erasure is a modeled, consensus-ordered event; six classes of authority fact survive it and the emergency path (the forensic floor); denials are recorded, though never ordered | OpenShell, AgentCore, AGT, OBPE and Mandato record denials, and two chain them; none models what survives erasure | 10.05 §4 item 5; ADR-0003, ADR-0006, ADR-0020, ADR-0028 |
| 6 | **Separation of duties as seats between principals, applied to the people** | Author/voter disjointness and recusal are in the action envelope; seat kinds bound alphabets; a person who operates and also votes is two principals (provisional) | Jing separates runtime roles; CaMeL and FIDES separate inside one agent; the Five Eyes guidance recommends such roles without a design | 10.05 §4 item 6; ADR-0024, ADR-0037, ADR-0050 |
| 7 | **Detection is deny-only, any-of, placed by rule and coupled to admission** | Observers sit off the Thinker's fault domain, combine with no quorum, and every effect-bearing Run admit needs a live Observer, so absence stalls | Sentry has a placement and no rule; monitors in the AI-control literature escalate rather than deny; no comparable couples admission to a detector's progress | 10.05 §4 item 7; ADR-0038, ADR-0039 |
| 8 | **Confinement as a ladder decided by failure direction** | Rungs are ordered by whether a host fault can add authority; attestation is a condition of the consensus grant; a principal below its declared rung is refused, never granted lower; `Contained` is a per-principal lookup naming each failing conjunct | Containment runtimes ship one rung; one research note uses the ladder vocabulary and decides rungs by Meta's Rule of Two, with no attestation | 10.05 §4 item 8; ADR-0034 |
| 9 | **The model is pinned as a promoted artifact, decoder included** | A Persona (weights, prompt, decoder) is attested at grant and renewal; lineage is projected from the log, not from the author's manifest (Proposed) | The IETF PTV draft attests model and policy; nobody pins the decoder | 10.05 §4 item 9; ADR-0016; ADR-0035 |
| 10 | **Monotonicity as a design-wide discipline** | Stated layer by layer at eighteen layers, from decision and policy to ingress, operation and evidence of proof | The field states it for one object at a time: policy tiers, labels, configuration scopes, privilege sets | 10.05 §4 item 10; ToO §7.8 |
| 11 | **Labels are declarations the mechanism delivers, and nothing reads content** | Every item of Run ingress is labelled by its declared source and delivered in the clear or as a handle with no bytes; three floor forbids bound what may go outward | CaMeL and FIDES trace per value, with a quarantined model or a framework-owned store in the agent's fault domain | 10.05 §4 item 11; ADR-0047 |
| 12 | **Semantic judgment can deny and never admit** | Only a syntactic lift reaches admit; classifiers, logprobs, Observer and human findings can only refuse; an evaluation that passes admits nothing | IBM's MCP guide puts guardrail models, LLM-as-judge and evaluation thresholds in the enforcement path; AGT's trust score can raise privilege | 10.09 Part IV; ADR-0007; ADR-0016 §2 |
| 13 | **Enumerated trust, not zero trust** | The trusted base is a declared table of thirty-seven rows under one admission rule, each with its class and residual; what can add authority and is fixed by nothing is refused | Zero trust distrusts users, devices and network position and still trusts the policy author; Moon & Varshney's trusted base is a five-item sentence | ADR-0045; 10.05 §2 A21 |
| 14 | **A computed theorem inventory** | A deployment declares a position against an archetype nobody ships, and `spec/inventory.py` computes which theorems its trusted base voids | No Playbook row or TACIP clause asks for it; the 10.02 review offered it to TACIP | 10.03 Playbook inventory §10 (stands at 10.09 §1.11); ADR-0023; ADR-0045 §3 |
| 15 | **The passive e-stop** | Lease expiry stops a principal when nobody acts; the fence is the active stop beside it; a lease is round-coterminous, so no privilege stands | Every MANAGE 2.4 suggestion presumes an actor who decides to act | 10.03 Playbook inventory §10; ADR-0031 §5 |
| 16 | **HOOTL is a distinct state** | Human-out-of-the-loop means no action was expressible, not that nobody acted; the three postures are `ENABLED` predicates | The Playbook has no row for it | 10.03 Playbook inventory §10; ToO §11.3 |
| 17 | **A tool never describes itself at run time** | The interface is part of the promoted allowlist entry, rendered by the mechanism as the round's opening record; a Run tool's one channel is declared once, and its network import takes no address | In MCP a tool supplies its own description, which the party it describes can change; interface pinning was declined in the protocol | 10.05 §0 item 2; ADR-0048, ADR-0049; ADR-0034 A2 |
| 18 | **History reaches policy only as mechanism folds** | `clearSet`, the sequence number and the round count are folds the mechanism computes and the mechanism's checks cover; policy declares none and has no temporal operator | Dogwood puts MFOTL in policy over a caller-named session; FORGE uses a Datalog monitor | 10.05 §0 item 3, §2 A15; ADR-0047 R13 |
| 19 | **The capability is removed, not mediated** | Build's six actions and Run's three remove the shell, the package manager, the network and git | OpenShell, `srt`, Codex, the Agent Harness and AGT keep them | 10.05 §6; ToO §2.4; ADR-0022, ADR-0040 |
| 20 | **An emergency elevates a named person for one round, never the agent** | A roster's quorum seats one person on an emergency seat whose envelope is computed from the log; no agent holds one (`NoAgentHoldsAnEmergencySeat`, proved); the floor keeps no exception for it (provisional) | The corpus's own earlier form gave an agent's seat more tools for an epoch; practice elsewhere elevates a person | ADR-0052; ADR-0013 A1 |
| 21 | **Policy is outside the verified artifact, bounded by a minimal verified floor** | Bundles change at runtime unverified; `admit ⟺ floor_permit ∧ ¬bundle_deny` makes every bundle a refinement of the floor by composition; the floor's evaluator is chosen for a mechanized semantics | Moon & Varshney place boundary policy inside the verified artifact and verify one fixed policy | ADR-0010, ADR-0030; ToO §11.1 |
| 22 | **The solver is trusted for nothing** | A routing `holds` counts only with a proof a pinned independent checker accepted; a `refuted` needs none, because it can only subtract | `tlapm`'s default trusts Z3's answer; Moon & Varshney trust a verifier that emits no certificate | ADR-0030 A1; ADR-0045 T-0045-14 |
| 23 | **Clocks buy liveness, never safety** | Epoch fencing carries safety without a clock; an interval is trusted by its failure direction; what is too fast to stop is removed from the alphabet | A synchronized-clock assumption, rejected as the one that fails quietly; Kubernetes and etcd leases rejected as the grant | ADR-0032 |
| 24 | **No bearer token crosses the guest channel** | At rung 1 the launcher is the channel; at rungs 2 and 3 the attested session key signs every action | — | ADR-0034 §3.5; ADR-0036 (Proposed) |
| 25 | **Collaboration, not autonomy** (Proposed) | No principal delegates; sub-agents exist only as cells of a promoted workflow; the orchestrator holds no state and may only narrow; termination is a bounded safety property with a budget that charges the orchestrator | Supervisors, handoffs, agent-as-tool and A2A let one model steer what runs next or call a subordinate with its own credentials; CaMeL's planner is trusted to write the plan | ADR-0054, ADR-0055; ToO §2.11 |
| 26 | **Detection as recomputable folds** (Proposed) | Five correlations over facts that a second reader can recompute, a judgment tier beside them, and a store that never outlives its sources | "A model reads everything and finds what it finds" | ADR-0051 |
| 27 | **The round is the unit, and persistent agent memory is outside the pattern** | The sealed round topic is the unit of work and erasure; `clearSet` is the only memory policy reads; a crashed round ends | IBM's guide treats memory as state and names memory poisoning | 10.09 Part IV; ToO §4.3 |

### 13.2 Process (11.2 in the 11.05 list; the row ids are carried)

| # | Characteristic | What TOPAK does | What the field does | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 28 | **A trust root kept in use with a recorded, and reported, defect** | The prover's two unsound cases are on its row, a rule and a scan keep proofs from them, every cited result was re-read, the reproducers are files, and the cases are reported upstream | No Playbook row asks what a verifier is known to prove falsely; the Playbook treats TEVV tooling as sound | 10.09 §1.11; ADR-0046 A2, A3; ADR-0045 A3, A4 |
| 29 | **A second deductive method whose purpose is to not share the first one's trusted base** | Lean re-proves fifteen proof modules' theorems by kernel-checked induction in place of the temporal backend, and certifies nothing new | No Playbook row contemplates a second TEVV route; Moon & Varshney have one verifier and trust it | 10.09 §1.11; ADR-0053 |
| 30 | **Checks aimed at a theorem that holds of a wrong model** | Non-vacuity by mutation and witness, gate controls that must fail, encodings checked against TLC's state graph, models of code replayed against recorded traces | Moon & Varshney's §4.6 lesson recommends such a check and does not build it | 10.09 Part III §3.1; ADR-0030 §5; ADR-0053 §2 |
| 31 | **Liveness stated as an extension of a frozen module** | A stall that is safe is told apart from a stall that ends; the fairness assumed becomes an assumption of use | MEASURE 2.6's "fails safely" has no vocabulary for the difference | 10.09 §1.11; ToO §5.5 |
| 32 | **A fixed verification vocabulary, none of it said of the running system** | Checked, proved, proved in Lean, certified, stated, built, simulated: each defined, never shortened, none meaning reviewed | The OpenAI mathematics tranche speaks of "different stages of verification" without defining them | ADR-0046 §1; ADR-0053 §1; `derived/topak-openai-math-tranche-2026.10.10.md` |
| 33 | **Self-attested, and says so on every face** | Independent review is a bootstrap precondition; every ratification row and every checkpoint states that none has happened | TACIP says self-attestation should not suppress a warning, and scores Verifiable as failing here | ADR-0029 A2; G11 §4; 10.09 Part II |
| 34 | **The design process is governed by the pattern's own rules** | Genesis custody declared, unilateral ratification with debt marked per criterion, provisional status with stated exits, the anchor's bootstrap conditioned on a review | The rejected alternatives are on ADR-0029's face: a forcing function, no confirmation class, the solo corpus called a degeneracy | ADR-0029; ToO §9 |
| 35 | **The reference is declared not to be the mechanism** | Never promoted, in no trusted base, failing its own first assumption of use, and every addendum says what it does not discharge; where it runs ahead of ratification, every surface says so | "The reference is the mechanism, promoted in place" is the rejected option | ADR-0033 §1, Options, A16, A17 |
| 36 | **The code pins the corpus** | Sixty-one digests of the records and modules the reference follows fail its build when one moves; every run is cited by manifest digest and commit | — | `mechanism/corpus-pins.sha256`; ADR-0033 |
| 37 | **Weak points and non-claims are published against itself** | Twenty-one places where the argument is thin, eighteen assumptions of use, twelve unanticipated results at G11, and a "what G11 does not claim" section | MEASURE 3.1 is marked partial for want of exactly this outside the corpus | ToO §12, §13; G11 §3, §8 |
| 38 | **The provenance of every choice is marked** | What the custodian settled in a design discussion is marked where it is used (⟨K1⟩ to ⟨K9⟩, ⟨M1⟩ to ⟨M5⟩); every other choice is the drafter's and says so | TACIP 12.2.3 asks for it tacitly; the alignment calls the markers unusual | G11 §5; 10.09 §2.4 |
| 39 | **A novel criterion is rehearsed on real pre-training before it may be ratified** (Proposed) | ADR-0035's lineage rule ran end to end on models trained from scratch, and a property check refuted the first draft's monotonicity claim | — | ADR-0035 §7, §8; `slm/` |
| 40 | **A security policy for a design-only corpus** | A wrong safety claim, a weak invariant or a bypassable floor policy is a security report | Conventional policies cover exploitable defects in running software | `SECURITY.md` |
| 41 | **The vocabulary is governed** | Thirty-eight named term collisions with rules, and every retired phrase with its replacement | — | glossary §13, §14 |

## 14. Where the field is ahead, or the pattern exposed

Carried from the 11.05 list. All twenty-three still stand at this tree (the review's register, §2, grades each
`open`). The overlay adds one the list did not have: it accepts delegation, Byzantine agreement and a deeper
trusted base, each of which the archetype had declined, and says so (ADR-0059 Consequences; ADR-0068 §4).

A distinguishing characteristic can be a liability, and the corpus says so against itself. Design-level only;
what is merely unbuilt is in G11 §8.

| # | Exposure | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **No refinement to running code.** Moon & Varshney reach deployed code by a checked refinement; TOPAK names the chain (T-0045-28) and has built stage one, trace validation, which is behind the tree at G11. The Lean proofs of the code are evidence toward it and not a stage | 10.05 §5 item 1; ADR-0045 T-0045-28, T-0045-30; ADR-0053 §5; G11 items 56, 103, 107 |
| 2 | **Nothing is independently reviewed, and there is no information barrier.** Every text since G10 was drafted by the assistant with the code in view; Moon & Varshney synthesize under a mutual-information barrier; no criterion covers assisted drafting | G11 §4, §8, item 66; 10.09 Part III §3.1 |
| 3 | **No delegation, where the field builds around chains.** Identity standards, vendor control planes and TACIP 13.2 assume an agent acts for another; TOPAK's answer, a promoted workflow, is Proposed | 10.05 §5 item 2, §6; ADR-0054 (Proposed) |
| 4 | **Provenance is per round, not per value.** Once any untrusted item is delivered clear, everything the Thinker emits inherits the round's labels; only suppression is finer | 10.05 §5 item 3; ADR-0047; ToO §12 item 18 |
| 5 | **History-dependent policy is a fixed set of folds.** "No more than N calls to tool T per round" needs a mechanism release or unanalysed Rego | 10.05 §5 item 4; ADR-0047 R13 |
| 6 | **Liveness is checked and not proved.** Four leads-to properties are TLC-checked at small constants; the promotion stall, a seal for every ended lease and detection are unmodelled | G11 §8, item 105; ToO §5.5 |
| 7 | **Durability.** A crashed round ends and its work is lost; Temporal, Restate and DBOS make survival of the worker the first question. The Adopter's Guide states it as a position; ToO §2.4 does not | 10.05 §5 item 6, §6; `ADOPTERS_GUIDE_README.md` §7 |
| 8 | **Latency and scale.** Every grant, heartbeat and effect admit waits for a consensus round, with no snapshots; gateways decide in microseconds | 10.05 §6; ToO §15 |
| 9 | **Expressiveness and incremental adoption.** The market has kept the shell in every product surveyed, and every comparable can be put in front of an existing agent; TOPAK's guarantees need the agent to speak its alphabet, and rung 0 gives attribution and authorization, not containment | 10.05 §6 |
| 10 | **Agent-assisted authoring is checked by a quorum, not in seconds** | 10.05 §6; ADR-0024 |
| 11 | **The operator and the emergency seat stand on provisional records.** A first lease still comes from whoever reaches a socket | 10.05 §5 item 7; G11 §8; ADR-0050, ADR-0052 |
| 12 | **The replicas are crash-recovery, and the transport is unauthenticated.** A compromised replica forges admits; mutual authentication is delegated and unbuilt | 10.05 §5 item 8; ADR-0045 T-0045-1; ToO §12 item 9 |
| 13 | **The trusted base is long, and longer**: thirty-seven rows against a five-item sentence. The trade is stated in ADR-0045 §2 | 10.05 §5 item 9; G11 §2.6 |
| 14 | **Floor fitness has an owner and no test, and no floor is ratified** | 10.05 §5 item 10; ADR-0029 A4; ADR-0024 E1; G11 item 87 |
| 15 | **The prover is unsound in two known ways, and no check detects the first.** Reported upstream and unfixed as of 2026.11.03 | ADR-0046 A2, A3; G11 item 104 |
| 16 | **Detection is unbuilt and fails open against a colluding Observer** | G11 §8; ADR-0039 §5; ADR-0051 (Proposed) |
| 17 | **A stall replaces escalation, and nothing alerts a person** | 10.09 §2.2; residual-risk statement 10.09 §1.14 |
| 18 | **Replay says what happened, never why**, and an emergency's reason is unknown by construction | ADR-0020; ADR-0052 §5; 10.09 §2.3 |
| 19 | **Containment is real for no principal.** Rung 1 trusts the host, rung 2 is a seam, rung 3 is SIMULATED, and the archetype needs hardware few can afford | G11 §8; ADR-0034 Consequences |
| 20 | **Neither timely nor measured.** `L` unmeasured, `T_irrev` undeclared, every value a placeholder, three more owed by the workflow records | G11 §8, item 101; 10.09 §1.7 |
| 21 | **Supply chain, secrets and operations.** Bundles signed under a throwaway key, gate evidence asserted, no SBOM, no reproducible build declared, no SLOs, no transport security at the reference | G11 §8; 10.09 Part IV; ADR-0045 T-0045-30 |
| 22 | **Evaluation and red-teaming exist nowhere, even as evidence** | 10.09 Part IV; ADR-0016 §2 |
| 23 | **No scope declaration and no deployment declaration**, and no known-good configuration of the whole: the reference follows text not in force | 10.09 Part III §3.1, §2.4; G11 §8, items 102, 103 |

## 15. Correspondence summary

The alignment's tally at G11, by function (`topak-external-alignment-2026.10.09.md` Part I):

| Function | Rows | M | P | C | G | X |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GOVERN | 19 | 3 | 9 | 0 | 0 | 7 |
| MAP | 18 | 4 | 3 | 1 | 1 | 9 |
| MEASURE | 22 | 2 | 7 | 1 | 2 | 10 |
| MANAGE | 13 | 2 | 8 | 1 | 0 | 2 |
| **Total** | **72** | **11** | **27** | **3** | **3** | **28** |

**What moved since G9.** Three rows, all up, each to a document or a record that did not exist at G9: GOVERN 1.2
(C → P, the architecture description's §10.1 mapping), MEASURE 2.13 (G → P, a TEVV process evaluated and found
defective, with a rule, a scan and a second method), MANAGE 1.4 (G → P, the residual-risk statement). Eleven rows
kept their mark and gained evidence (alignment §1.5). Two gaps grew while keeping their mark: MEASURE 1.3
(twenty-nine confirmation-required records, every text since G10 assistant-drafted, no reviewer) and MEASURE 3.2
(three placeholders added by the workflow records).

**What Batches 38 and 39 would move.** The marks above are the alignment's and are not re-made here. The alignment's
own list of what to do next (§1.12) has since been acted on in three places. Its item 2, the upstream report of the
prover's two cases, which "three rows ask for in as many words", was made on 2026.10.18 and ratified as ADR-0046 A3
with ADR-0045 A4 (Batch 39): GOVERN 4.3, MANAGE 4.3 and the sharing clause of MEASURE 2.7 lose the half the alignment
held against them, and MANAGE 3.1's "the defects unreported to the third party" no longer holds; the reverse delta
"a trust root kept in use with a recorded defect" (§1.11) now also has the defect reported. Its item 3, a
residual-risk statement against G11, was written the same day as the alignment (`topak-residual-risk-statement-2026.10.09.md`),
so MANAGE 1.4, MEASURE 1.1 and GOVERN 4.2 hold their P with a current statement rather than a stale one. Its item 6,
ADR-0024's miscitation of Moon & Varshney, "blocking for any external draft", is corrected by ADR-0024 E1 (Batch 38).
Items 1 (a reviewer), 4 (floor fitness defined, a measurement program, a proficiency declaration) and 5 (the three
convertible rows as one appendix) stand.

**By principle.** P2 and P6 still carry MANAGE, now with proofs on both sides of the emergency path
(`EmergencyNeverCoupled`, `EmergencyCallsWithoutObserver`; the grant bounded to one round). P1 carries the TEVV rows
and is where MEASURE 2.13 moved: the one TEVV process the corpus evaluated was its own prover. P8 is where GOVERN 1.4,
MEASURE 2.8 and MANAGE 4.3's tracking half land, and is also the evidence for the row that is largest: MEASURE 1.3,
everything one hand's. P7 is still where MEASURE 2.4 lands and still the principle with the most open features, and
since G11 the one with liveness evidence beside safety. P9 moved no Playbook row, and labels appear in none of the
alignment's row notes. P10 appears in the notes of five rows (GOVERN 1.6, MAP 3.3, MEASURE 3.2, MANAGE 1.1 and 3.2)
and moved none, because its records are Proposed.

**What the Proposed records would move.** No mark is re-made here, and the Playbook was not re-read. The
review (`topak-critical-review-2026.11.11.md` §2) lists the rows the thirteen records would bear on if ratified: MAP 3.4
and MEASURE 3.2 would grow as gaps (a person at a release seat with nothing on proficiency; `K_up`, the tick
period, the capture floor and the budgets as new undeclared numbers), MEASURE 1.3 grows today (thirteen more
records, one hand), and MANAGE 2.4, MAP 3.5, GOVERN 1.6 and MEASURE 2.10 would each gain evidence without a
change of mark.

## 16. Playbook practices not addressed

The alignment's §1.7 to §1.10 carry the lists: the three gaps (MAP 3.4 operator proficiency, MEASURE 1.3 independent
assessors, MEASURE 3.2 tracking where metrics are unavailable), with floor fitness carried beside them under its own
name; the three convertible rows (MAP 3.2, MEASURE 2.10, MANAGE 2.1); the twenty-seven partial rows with their
unaddressed half; and the twenty-eight out-of-scope rows by group. They are not repeated here.

## 17. Movement since the 11.05 list

**The carried rows.** 33 rows moved after the 11.05 list's tree and 30 were already untrue at it.
The moves have three causes: ADR-0057's three pending amendments in accepted records, the overlay's eight modules
changing every count of proof modules, and ADR-0056 and ADR-0058 touching the lease and the degeneracy relation.
The corrections are mostly rows that said *Built* where the reference has no code path (erasure, retention,
replay authorization, masking, the legibility horizon, the correlator's store), and rows that took an evidence
word from the record's module and not from the feature's own clause.

| F1.8 | moved | two pending amendments of 2026.11.07 restate T-0045-28 as a derived premise | basis, status | decisions/ADR-0045.md:383; decisions/ADR-0045.md:149; decisions/ADR-0045.md:531; decisions/ADR-0053.md:284; decisions/ADR-0053.md:493 … |

## 18. Stale sentences in the sources

Nothing here is a finding against a record's decision text. Each is a sentence in a living file, a status
cell or a comment that the tree has moved past. **All twenty-two of the 11.05 list's are still there**: no cut has
followed, and each was confirmed at this tree (the review's register, §2). They are carried first.
